Experimental setups\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.1. Dataset\\r\\n\\r\\nReal-world WDSs typically lack comprehensive nodal monitoring, preventing direct validation of demand forecasts at non-instrumented nodes. To address this limitation and assess model performance under realistic conditions, this study adopt the benchmark l-Town Network dataset [30]. As depicted in Fig. 2(a), this network has substantial elevation variations, with a maximum difference of 74.2 m, and is serviced by two water tanks. It includes notable high and low areas where water pressure is regulated by a pressurized pumping station and a pressure-reducing valve, respectively. This network essentially covers the characteristics found in typical real WDSs.\\r\\n\\r\\nImage: Fig 2\\r\\n\\r\\n  1. Download: Download high-res image (1MB)\\r\\n  2. Download: Download full-size image\\r\\n\\r\\nFig. 2. Nodal features of l-Town Network. (a) Nodal height features (b) Nodal type features (c) Variation of different water usage types over a year.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe l-Town benchmark network comprises 50 predefined residential and commercial water demand patterns, along with 4 industrial demand patterns. To enhance the modelâ€™s relevance to real-world scenarios, this study slightly modified the l-Town network. Specifically, this study randomly assigned these 104 demand patterns to the 782 demand nodes. Fig. 2(b) depicts the water usage type at each demand node; Fig. 2(c) illustrates all water demand pattern curves, with grey lines representing individual patterns and the red line indicating their mean values. A total of one year of sequence data with a time step of 5 min was generated. The training, validation, and testing datasets were constructed by chronologically assigning 70 %, 20 %, and 10 % of the total samples, respectively.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pressurized pumping station that provides additional pressure to the high zone is represented as a node with known pressures for prediction. Its nodal pressures were obtained from pump characteristic curves and flow sensors installed on the pump. In the weighted adjacency matrix, the two pipelines connected to the â€œpumping station nodeâ€ are represented by a sufficiently large number (300 in this example), while the pressure-reducing valve is represented by a sufficiently small number (1 in this example).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.2. Model settings\\r\\n\\r\\nFor the forecasting tasks, this study adopted the GHR proposed by Zhou et al. [37] and the GGNN used by Li et al. [18] to effectively compare our proposed AGN model with previous studies. Especially, this study combined the GHRâ€™s state estimation results with the GRU time prediction algorithm (GHR+GRU) to achieve forecasting task and extended the GGNN from water quality to the task of nodal demand.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor all three models (AGN, GGNN, and GRU), the hidden layer dimension was set to 12. All three neural networks were trained using the ADAM optimizer with a training batch size of 32. The initial learning rate was set to 1e-3, and it was reduced by a factor of 0.8 after 1 training epoch. If the training error does not decrease over 10 batches, the epoch will be terminated early. For the AGN and GGNN, the number of recurrent layers was set to 3. The forecasting step length is set to be equal to the entered historical step length.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.3. Evaluation metric\\r\\n\\r\\nThis study reported the Mean Absolute Error (MAE) and Mean Absolute Percentage Error (MAPE) of forecasting results for all 783 nodes (including 1 â€œpumping station nodeâ€) in different forecasting time steps, to evaluate the model's spatial generalization and time series forecasting effect. name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter
from pypdf.generic import NameObject

reader = PdfReader(input_pdf)
writer = PdfWriter()
writer.clone_document_from_reader(reader)

# Restores widgets that are missing from /AcroForm/Fields.
writer.reattach_fields()
fields = writer.get_fields() or {}
missing = set(expected_values) - set(fields)
if missing:
    raise ValueError(f"Form fields not found after repair: {sorted(missing)}")

values_to_write = dict(expected_values)
if flatten:
    # Paint every existing value before removing every widget.
    values_to_write = {
        name: field.get("/V", "/Off" if field.get("/FT") == "/Btn" else "")
        for name, field in fields.items()
    }
    values_to_write.update(expected_values)

writer.update_page_form_field_values(
    None, values_to_write, auto_regenerate=False, flatten=flatten
)

if flatten:
    # pypdf's flatten=True paints appearances but does not remove widgets.
    writer.remove_annotations(subtypes="/Widget")
    writer.root_object.pop(NameObject("/AcroForm"), None)

with open(output_pdf, "wb") as stream:
    writer.write(stream)
```

4. Reopen the written PDF before delivery. For an interactive result, require every expected field to be present in `get_fields()` with the expected `/V`, enumerate page widgets again, and confirm their effective `/V` (the widget value or inherited `/Parent` value) agrees. Confirm each updated widget has a non-empty `/AP` `/N` appearance and render the final pages to catch stale or clipped appearances. Do not rely on `/NeedAppearances` or a successful PNG render as proof that logical field data was updated.
5. For a flattened result, require zero `/Widget` annotations and no remaining `/AcroForm` field tree after reopening, then render the final pages. Keep an editable copy when the user may need to revise the form.

## Temp And Output Conventions

- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies

Prefer the Codex bundled workspace/runtime dependencies when available. The primary runtime is expected to include:

- Python packages: `reportlab`, `pdfplumber`, `pypdf`
- Rendering tools: `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo` from Poppler

If a dependency is missing, install only what is needed.

Python packages:

```bash
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

If `uv` is unavailable:

```bash
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

System tools for rendering:

```bash
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment

No required environment variables.

## Rendering Command

```bash
pdftoppm -png "$INPUT_PDF" "$OUTPUT_PREFIX"
```

## Quality Expectations

- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final Checks

- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers, footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

## Final response

### Final Response Citations

- **Create/edit:** for created or edited PDFs, cite each final deliverable exactly once with `purpose="output"`, in its own paragraph. Briefly describe the result without repeating its filename, path, or link. Never put output directives inside prose or list items.
- **Q&A and summaries:** Every file whose contents you summarize or use to support an answer MUST receive a `:codex-file-citation{...}` with `purpose="source"` in the final response. Use the source citation in place of the filename, wherever the filename would appear. Do not write the filename separately and append its citation at the end. Files merely inspected but not used in the answer need no citation. Do not emit output citations for unchanged files or read-only answers.

- Emit `:codex-file-citation{...}` directly, without backticks, code fences, or a Markdown-link wrapper. Use verified targets only. No need to cite or reference intermediate work.

For file citations, set `path` to the verified absolute file path on the task host. Omit `mode`.

Some illustrative examples, real responses use verified targets outside code fences.

```text
:codex-file-citation{path="/absolute/path/review.pdf" purpose="output"}
```

```text
The review of :codex-file-citation{path="/absolute/path/review.pdf" purpose="source"} is due Friday.
```


### Additional Citation Guidance

For PDF Q&A, inspect complete relevant pages and preserve material headings, table/figure labels, footnotes, sources, and sample sizes. PDF citations support only plain file citations; do not add `artifact_kind`, `page_number`, or other locators.

def tex(s):
 return ''.join({'\\':r'\textbackslash{}','&':r'\&','%':r'\%','$':r'\$','#':r'\#','_':r'\_','{':r'\{','}':r'\}','~':r'\textasciitilde{}','^':r'\textasciicircum{}'}.get(c,c) for c in s)
class HTMLTex(HTMLParser):
 def __init__(self):super().__init__();self.parts=[]
 def handle_starttag(self,tag,attrs):
  attr=dict(attrs)
  if tag=='b':self.parts.append(r'\textbf{')
  elif tag=='font':self.parts.append(r'\texttt{')
  elif tag=='link':self.parts.append(r'\href{'+attr['href'].replace('%',r'\%').replace('#',r'\#')+'}{')
  elif tag=='br':self.parts.append(r'\par ')
 def handle_endtag(self,tag):
  if tag in ['b','font','link']:self.parts.append('}')
 def handle_data(self,s):self.parts.append(internal_cites(s))
reference_numbers={int(re.match(r'^\[(\d+)\]',label)[1]) for label,url in data['references']}
def internal_cites(s):
 if not data.get('internal_citations'):return tex(s)
 parts=[];last=0
 for m in re.finditer(r'\[(\d+(?:,\s*\d+)*)\]',s):
  parts.append(tex(s[last:m.start()]))
  nums=[int(n) for n in m[1].split(',')]
  assert all(n in reference_numbers for n in nums),m[0]
  parts.append('['+','.join(r'\hyperlink{ref:'+str(n)+'}{'+str(n)+'}' for n in nums)+']')
  last=m.end()
 parts.append(tex(s[last:]));return ''.join(parts)

def html(s):p=HTMLTex();p.feed(s);return ''.join(p.parts)
def table(rows,widths):
 n=len(widths);ratios=[w/sum(widths)*n for w in widths]
 cols=''.join(r'>{\hsize='+f'{r:.5f}'+r'\hsize\raggedright\arraybackslash}X' for r in ratios)
 return '\n'.join([r'{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.16}',r'\rowcolors{2}{PaleBlue}{white}',r'\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}'+cols+r'@{}}',r'\rowcolor{Blue!13} '+' & '.join(r'\textbf{'+html(c)+'}' for c in rows[0])+r' \\',*[' & '.join((r'\textbf{\color{Navy}'+html(c)+'}' if i==0 else html(c)) for i,c in enumerate(row))+r' \\' for row in rows[1:]],r'\end{tabularx}\par}',r'\vspace{1.7mm}'])

  elif kind=='math':parts.append(r'\['+args[0]+r'\]')

"""Validate external references, own descriptive numbers and exact PDF citation targets."""
from pathlib import Path
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
import csv,hashlib,json,re,urllib.request,urllib.parse
import fitz,numpy as np,pandas as pd
W=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1];M=W.parent/'MiniProject/Milestone 2'
O=M/'respaldo/estado_del_arte/lectura_critica_v023'
def sha(p):return hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
def dump(p,d):p.write_text(json.dumps(d,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\n',encoding='utf-8')
d=json.loads((W/'templates/milestone2_content.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
headers={'User-Agent':'DS675 academic reference verification/1.0','Accept':'application/json,text/html'}
def get(url,json_mode=True):
 with urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request(url,headers=headers),timeout=18) as r:
  data=r.read();return (json.loads(data) if json_mode else data),r.status,r.url
def check(row):
 label,url=row;number=int(re.match(r'^\[(\d+)\]',label)[1]);item={'number':number,'label':label,'url':url}
 try:
  if 'doi.org/' in url:
   doi=url.split('doi.org/',1)[1];meta,status,target=get('https://api.crossref.org/works/'+urllib.parse.quote(doi,safe=''))
   record=meta['message'];assert record['DOI'].lower()==doi.lower()
   item.update(verified=True,method='Crossref registered DOI record',metadata_status=status,registered_title=record['title'][0],registered_authors=[r.get('family','') for r in record.get('author',[])],registry_url=target)
  elif 'zenodo.org/records/' in url:
   rid=url.rsplit('/',1)[1];meta,status,target=get('https://zenodo.org/api/records/'+rid)
   assert str(meta['id'])==rid
   item.update(verified=True,method='Original Zenodo record API',metadata_status=status,registered_title=meta['metadata']['title'],registered_authors=[r['name'] for r in meta['metadata'].get('creators',[])],registry_url=target)
  elif 'github.com/' in url:
   parts=urllib.parse.urlparse(url).path.strip('/').split('/');owner,repo=parts[:2];commit=parts[3]
   meta,status,target=get('https://api.github.com/repos/'+owner+'/'+repo+'/commits/'+commit)
   assert meta['sha']==commit
   item.update(verified=True,method='Original GitHub commit API',metadata_status=status,commit=commit,registry_url=target)
  else:
   data,status,target=get(url,False);assert status==200
   assert any(name in data.decode('utf-8',errors='replace') for name in ['Ridge','RandomForestRegressor','GradientBoostingRegressor'])
   item.update(verified=True,method='Official documentation HTTP content',status=status,resolved_url=target)
 except Exception as e:item.update(verified=False,error=type(e).__name__+': '+str(e))
 return item
cached=O/'external_reference_check.json'
previous=json.loads(cached.read_text(encoding='utf-8')) if cached.exists() else None
if previous and previous.get('all_verified') and [r['url'] for r in previous['references']]==[url for label,url in d['references']]:
 references=previous['references']
 for row,(label,url) in zip(references,d['references']):row['label']=label
else:
 with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=6) as pool:references=list(pool.map(check,d['references']))
dump(O/'external_reference_check.json',{'references':references,'self_domain_references':0,'all_verified':all(r['verified'] for r in references)})
print(json.dumps({'external_references':len(references),'verified':sum(r['verified'] for r in references),'failures':[r for r in references if not r['verified']]},ensure_ascii=False))
assert all(r['verified'] for r in references),'External reference verification incomplete'

C=M/'respaldo/datos/oficial/csv_verificados';frames={};struct=[]
for year in [2018,2019]:
 for kind,n in [('Pressures',33),('Flows',3),('Levels',1),('Demands',82)]:

  assert (f.index.to_series().diff().dropna()==pd.Timedelta(minutes=5)).all()
  struct.append({'file':path.name,'shape':list(f.shape),'missing':0,'sha256':sha(path),'scope':'structure only' if year==2019 else 'structure plus Jan-Sep descriptive EDA'})
  if year==2018:frames[kind]=f.loc['2018-01-01':'2018-09-30']
p=frames['Pressures'];x=pd.DataFrame({n:p[n] for n in ['n1','n4','n31']})
x['T1']=frames['Levels'].T1;x['PUMP_1']=frames['Flows'].PUMP_1;x['AMR_n1']=frames['Demands'].n1
x['n1 +15']=p.n1.shift(-3);pairs=x.dropna();err=pairs['n1 +15']-pairs.n1
eda={'rows':len(p),'days':len(p.index.normalize().unique()),'pairs':len(pairs),'MAE':float(err.abs().mean()),'RMSE':float(np.sqrt((err**2).mean())),'range':[float(p.n1.min()),float(p.n1.max())],'correlations':{c:float(pairs[c].corr(pairs['n1 +15'])) for c in ['n1','T1','PUMP_1','AMR_n1']}}
assert eda['rows']==78624 and eda['days']==273 and eda['pairs']==78621
assert abs(eda['MAE']-.0517762429885)<1e-12 and eda['range']==[25.67,29.36]

   internal.append({'source_page':page_no+1,'reference_number':n,'destination_page':5,'anchor_matches_bibliographic_label':True})
  all_links.append(link)
assert {l['url'] for l in external}==set(refs.values())
